L2376: ## **Study design and methodology:**
L2377: Study CLNP023C12301 was a multicenter, single-arm, open-label trial which is comprised of 8 weeks screening period, 24-week core treatment period and 24-week extension treatment period.
L2378: Eligible PNH patients with hemolysis (LDH &gt; 1.5 ULN) and anemia (hemoglobin &lt;10 g/dL), who were naive to complement inhibitor therapy, including anti-C5 antibody treatment, patients received iptacopan monotherapy at a dose 200 mg orally b.i.d
L2379: **Number of patients planned:** A total of 40 patients were planned. All patients provided written informed consent prior to the start of any study-related activities.
L2380: **Number of patients analyzed:** A total of 40 patients were enrolled and started study treatment in the trial.
L2381: A treatment duration of 24 weeks is considered appropriate to assess the effect of iptacopan on the primary and secondary efficacy endpoints, as well as on safety and tolerability.
L2382: ## **Diagnosis and main criteria for inclusion and exclusion:**
L2383: The study population consisted of PNH patients with hemolysis (LDH &gt; 1.5 ULN) and anemia (hemoglobin &lt;10 g/dL), who were naive to complement inhibitor therapy.
L2384: Key inclusion criteria included:
L2385: - Male and female patients ≥ 18 years of age with a diagnosis of PNH confirmed by high-sensitivity flow cytometry with RBCs and WBCs (granulocyte/monocyte) clone size ≥ 10%.
- Mean hemoglobin level &lt;10 g/dL confirmed by central laboratory assessment during Screening and prior to starting study treatment:By two hemoglobin measurements (mean &lt; 10 g/dL), 2 to 8 weeks apart, for patients not receiving a RBC transfusion during Screening.
- By one hemoglobin measurement (&lt;10 g/dL) carried at the first Screening visit for patients receiving a RBC transfusion after which he/she will be eligible.
- LDH &gt; 1.5 x Upper Limit of Normal (ULN) for at least 2 central laboratory measurements 2 to 8 weeks apart during the screening period.
L2386: ## NDA 218276
## FABHALTA (iptacopan) capsules
L2387: - Vaccination against *Neisseria meningitidis* infection was required prior to initiation of iptacopan treatment. If iptacopan treatment was started less than 2 weeks post-vaccination or before a specific vaccination was given, prophylactic antibiotic was to be given to the patient at the start of iptacopan and for at least 2 weeks after vaccination.
- If not received previously, vaccination against *Streptococcus pneumoniae* and *Haemophilus influenzae* should be given (if available and according to local/national regulations), and are recommended at least 2 weeks prior to initiation of iptacopan treatment. However, administration of these vaccines less than 2 weeks prior to start of iptacopan treatment or up to 2 weeks (up to Day 14) after iptacopan initiation, was at the discretion of the investigator. If iptacopan treatment was started less than 2 weeks post-vaccination or before a specific vaccination was given, patient had to be given prophylactic antibiotic at the start of iptacopan and for at least 2 weeks after vaccination.
L2388: ## Key exclusion criteria included:
L2389: - Prior treatment with a complement inhibitor, including anti-C5 antibody.
- Known or suspected hereditary complement deficiency.
- History of hematopoietic stem cell transplantation.
- Patients with laboratory evidence of bone marrow failure (reticulocytes &lt;100 \times 10^9/L; platelets &lt;30 \times 10^9/L; neutrophils &lt;0.5 \times 10^9/L).
- Active systemic bacterial, viral (including COVID-19) or fungal infection within 14 days prior to study drug administration.
- A history of recurrent invasive infections caused by encapsulated organisms, e.g., meningococcus or pneumococcus.
- Major concurrent comorbidities including but not limited to severe kidney disease, advanced cardiac disease, severe pulmonary disease, or hepatic disease that in the opinion of the investigator precluded patient's participation in the study.
- Concomitant use of any of the following medications is prohibited if not on a stable regimen for the time period indicated below prior to Screening:Erythropoiesis-stimulating agents (ESAs) for at least 8 weeks.
- Any immunosuppressants for at least 8 weeks.
- Systemic corticosteroids given for hematological conditions (less than 0.25 mg/kg/d) for at least 4 weeks.
- Vitamin K antagonists (e.g., warfarin) with a stable international normalized ratio (INR) for at least 4 weeks.
- Low-molecular-weight heparin, and the direct oral anticoagulants rivaroxaban, apixaban and edoxaban, for at least 4 weeks.
- Iron supplements, vitamin B12, or folic acid for at least 4 weeks.
- Hypoxia-inducible factor prolyl hydroxylase inhibitors (HIF-PHIs) such as roxadustat for at least 8 weeks.
- Androgens for at least 4 weeks.
- Women of child-bearing potential, defined as all women physiologically capable of becoming pregnant, unless they are using effective methods of contraception during dosing of investigational drug and for 1 week after stopping of investigational drug.
L2390: ## 16. Efficacy
L2391: ## 16.1. APPLY-PNH: Exploratory Efficacy Endpoint Results
L2392: The efficacy results described below were reported by the Applicant. These results are exploratory and do not support any labeling claims.
L2393: ## Erythrocytes
L2394: At baseline, the mean (SD) erythrocyte count was comparable and below the lower limit of normal (normal reference range: 4.1 to 5.9\times 10^{12}/\text{L} for males; 3.8 to 5.9\times 10^{12}/\text{L} for females) for both the iptacopan group (2.50\times 10^{12}/\text{L} (0.34)) and the anti-C5 group (2.43\times 10^{12}/\text{L} (0.41)). In the iptacopan group, an increase in the mean (SD) erythrocyte count was seen between day 7 (3.03 (0.35))\times 10^{12}/\text{L}) and day 42 (3.75 (0.45))\times 10^{12}/\text{L}). From day 56 until day 168 (3.72 (0.50))\times 10^{12}/\text{L}), the mean (SD) erythrocyte count was stable. In the anti-C5 group, no trend was seen, and the mean erythrocyte count remained consistently low through day 168.
L2395: ## Erythrocyte Distribution Width
L2396: At baseline, the mean (SD) erythrocyte distribution width was comparable and above the upper limit of normal ([ULN]; normal reference range: 11.6 to 14.8%) for both the iptacopan group (19.36% (4.63)) and anti-C5 group (20.36% (5.55)). In the iptacopan group, a reduction in mean (SD) erythrocyte distribution width was observed at day 28 (15.94% (3.39)). Thereafter, mean erythrocyte distribution width was stable until day 168 (15.18% (1.82)). In the anti-C5 group, the mean erythrocyte distribution width remained consistently high through day 168.
L2397: ## Haptoglobin
L2398: Mean (SD) haptoglobin at baseline was comparable and below the lower limit of normal (normal reference range: 0.3 to 2 g/L) for both the iptacopan group (0.12 g/L (0.09)) and the anti-C5 group (0.11 g/L (0.05)). The lower limit of quantification for haptoglobin is &lt;0.2 g/L (central laboratory). In the iptacopan group, an increase in haptoglobin was seen on day 28, with a mean (SD) haptoglobin of 0.34 g/L (0.28). From day 56 onwards, mean haptoglobin was below the lower limit of normal until day 168, at which point the mean (SD) haptoglobin was 0.15 g/L (0.240) and below the lower limit of quantification. In the anti-C5 group, mean haptoglobin remained consistently low through day 168.
L2399: ## Indirect Bilirubin
L2400: Mean (SD) indirect bilirubin at baseline was comparable and above the ULN (normal reference range: 2 to 17 \mu\text{mol}/\text{L}) for both the iptacopan group (23.4 \mu\text{mol}/\text{L} (28.28)) and the anti-C5 group (22.6 \mu\text{mol}/\text{L} (19.92)). The median (range) indirect bilirubin was comparable between the iptacopan group (15.0 \mu\text{mol}/\text{L} (3 to 152)) and the anti-C5 group (16.0 \mu\text{mol}/\text{L} (2 to 99)). In the iptacopan group, a reduction in the mean and median indirect bilirubin values was observed as early as day 7. Thereafter, mean and median indirect bilirubin were stable until day 168, at which point the mean (SD) indirect bilirubin was 7.6 \mu\text{mol}/\text{L} (7.65) and median (range) indirect bilirubin was 5.0 \text{mmol}/\text{L} (0 to 46). In the iptacopan group, the mean and median total bilirubin
L2401: values were within normal range from day 7 through the end of the randomized treatment period. In the anti-C5 group, mean (SD) indirect bilirubin between baseline (22.6 \mumol/L (19.92)) and postbaseline until day 168 (18.8 \mumol/L (13.58)) remained consistently high.
L2402: ## Number and Units of RBC Transfusions
L2403: Five subjects (8.1%) in the iptacopan group and 19 (54.3%) in the anti-C5 group received at least one transfusion. Among the subjects who received transfusions, the mean (SD) number of transfusions per subject was 1.4 (0.89) in the iptacopan group and 4.9 (3.97) in the anti-C5 group. Among the subjects who were transfused, the mean (SD) number of units of red blood cell (RBC) transfused per subject was 2.2 (1.64) in the iptacopan group and 8.2 (6.73) in the anti-C5 group. Among the subjects who were transfused, the median (range) number of units of RBC transfused per subject was 2.0 (1 to 5) units in the iptacopan group and 7.0 (1 to 28) units in the anti-C5 group.
L2404: ## PNH Signs and Symptoms
L2405: Overall, 39/62 (62.9%) subjects in the iptacopan group and 24/35 (68.6%) subjects in the anti-C5 group reported PNH signs and symptoms at baseline. At the end of the randomized treatment period, 15/62 (24.2%) subjects in the iptacopan group and 20/35 (57.1%) in the anti-C5 group reported at least one PNH-related sign and symptom.
L2406: Feeling weak or tired was the most frequently reported event. At baseline, 48.4% of subjects in the iptacopan group reported none, while 29%, 19.4%, and 3.2% reported mild, moderate, and severe events, respectively. In the anti-C5 group, 31.4% of subjects reported none, and 34.3%, 25.7%, and 5.7% reported mild, moderate, and severe weakness and fatigue at baseline, respectively. On day 168, 80.6% of subjects in the iptacopan group reported none, 14.5% reported mild, 4.8% reported moderate, and 0 reported severe weakness or fatigue, while 45.7% of subjects reported none, 31.4% reported mild, 17.1% reported moderate, and 5.7% reported severe weakness or fatigue in the anti-C5 group.
L2407: At baseline, hemoglobinuria was reported for 83.9% of subjects as none, 11.3% as mild, and 4.8% as moderate in the iptacopan group, whereas hemoglobinuria was reported in 85.7% of subjects as none, 8.6% as moderate, and 2.9% as severe in the anti-C5 group. On day 168, this changed to 100% reported as none in the iptacopan group versus 85.7% reported as none, 8.6% as mild, and 5.7% as moderate in the anti-C5 group.
L2408: At baseline, shortness of breath was reported for 71% of subjects as none, 14.5% as mild, 12.9% as moderate, and 1.6% as severe in the iptacopan group, whereas shortness of breath was reported as none for 62.9% of subjects, as mild for 20%, as moderate for 11.4%, and as severe for 2.9% in the anti-C5 group. On day 168, shortness of breath was reported for 93.5% of subjects as none, 4.8% as mild, and 1.6% as moderate in the iptacopan group whereas it was reported for 71.4% of subjects as none, 5.7% as mild, 17.1% as moderate, and 5.7% as severe in the anti-C5 group.
L2409: ## PNH Clone Size
L2410: Mean (SD) total PNH RBC clone size at baseline was slightly higher in the iptacopan group (64.65% (27.45)) than that in the anti-C5 group (57.39% (29.73)). In the iptacopan group, an increase in mean (SD) total PNH RBC clone size was observed from day 28 (83.48% (16.47)) to
L2411: NDA 218276
FABHALTA (iptacopan) capsules
L2412: day 112 (93.39% (9.82)), with the mean (SD) thereafter stable until day 168 (93.17% (10.72)). In the anti-C5 group, mean (SD) of total PNH RBC clone size was stable from baseline (57.39% (29.73)) until day 168 (59.68% (26.71)).
L2413: ## **Clinical Reviewer Comment:**
L2414: *Overall, markers of hemolysis (except for haptoglobin) and transfusion burden improved with iptacopan therapy. The findings on PNH signs and symptoms are difficult to interpret in an open-label trial.*
L2415: ## 16.2. APPOINT-PNH: Secondary Efficacy Endpoint Results
L2416: The efficacy results described below were reported by the Applicant. These results were exploratory and do not support any labeling claims.
L2417: ## **Hemoglobin Levels \ge 12 g/dL in Absence of Red Blood Cell Transfusion**
L2418: The proportion of subjects who achieved hemoglobin (Hb) levels of \ge 12 g/dL in the absence of RBC transfusions was 19/33 (62.8%) between day 14 and day 168. The total number of subjects in the study was 40; however, only 33 subjects had nonmissing data.
L2419: ## **Absence of Administration of Packed Red Blood Cell Transfusions**
L2420: Between day 14 and day 168, 40/40 subjects (97.6%) avoided transfusion. Twenty-eight subjects (70.0%) received at least one transfusion in the 6 months prior to start of study treatment.
L2421: ## **Change From Baseline in Hb**
L2422: Mean (SD) Hb at baseline was 8.16 (1.09) g/dL and median (range) Hb at baseline was 8.05 g/dL (5.80 to 10.00). The adjusted mean change from baseline in Hb between day 126 and day 168 was +4.28 g/dL (95% CI: 3.87, 4.70). In subjects treated with iptacopan, the increases in Hb levels were seen early in the core treatment period, with an adjusted mean change from baseline (95% CI) in Hb of 0.74 g/dL (0.31, 1.17) on day 7. A further increase in Hb was observed on day 14, with an adjusted mean change from baseline (95% CI) in Hb of 1.51 g/dL (1.06, 1.96). At each visit from day 28 until day 168, the adjusted mean change from baseline in Hb level was &gt;2 g/dL.
L2423: ## **Percent Change From Baseline in LDH**
L2424: Baseline mean (SD) LDH was 1698.8 (683.33) U/L and the median (range) LDH was 1581.5 U/L (522 to 3244 U/L). The ULN for LDH, for the Applicant, was 250 U/L. After treatment with iptacopan, there was an adjusted mean percent change from baseline in LDH levels between day 126 and day 168 of -83.55% (95% CI -84.90%, -82.08%). The adjusted mean percent change from baseline (95% CI) in LDH was -70.11% (-72.11, -67.97) on day 7 and greater than -83% (-84.55, -86.37) at any visit after day 7 in the core treatment period.
L2425: ## **Occurrences of Breakthrough Hemolysis**
L2426: In the treatment period, none of the subjects experienced clinical breakthrough hemolysis. One subject reported clinical breakthrough hemolysis in the extension period. The subject was hospitalized with coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) at the time.
L2427: ## **Change From Baseline in Reticulocyte Count**
L2428: Baseline mean (SD) absolute reticulocyte count was 154.33 (63.66) \times 10^9/L, and the median (range) absolute reticulocyte count was 139.20 (59.4 \text{ to } 324.8) \times 10^9/L. The ULN for absolute reticulocyte count is 123 \times 10^9/L. The adjusted mean (95% CI) change from baseline in absolute reticulocyte count between day 126 and day 168 was -82.48 \times 10^9/L (-89.33, -75.62). In subjects treated with iptacopan, decreases in absolute reticulocyte count were seen as early as day 7 in the core treatment period, with an adjusted mean (95% CI) change from baseline of -85.75 \times 10^9/L (-93.16, -78.35). There was a further decrease in absolute reticulocyte count on day 14 with an adjusted mean change from baseline of -91.23 \times 10^9/L (-96.94, -85.53). The maximum effect was on day 28, with an adjusted mean (95% CI) change from baseline of -93.04 \times 10^9/L (-100.43, -85.65). At each visit from day 42 until day 168, the adjusted mean change from baseline in absolute reticulocyte count ranged between -78.99 \times 10^9/L and &lt; -87.46 \times 10^9/L.
L2429: ## **Change From Baseline in FACIT Fatigue Scores**
L2430: Each of the 13 items of the FACIT-Fatigue scale ranges from 0 to 4; the range of possible scores is 0 to 52, with 0 being the worst possible score and 52 the best. The mean (SD) FACIT-Fatigue score at baseline was 32.78 (10.17) points. The adjusted mean (95% CI) FACIT-Fatigue score change from baseline between day 126 and day 168 was +10.75 (8.66, 12.84) points. On day 168, the mean (SD) FACIT-Fatigue score reached 43.9 (6.24) points. There was an increase in the adjusted mean change from baseline in FACIT-Fatigue scores from day 7 (+3.26 points) until day 84 (+10.30 points). At each visit from day 126 until day 168, the adjusted mean change from baseline in FACIT-Fatigue score was \ge 9.91 points.
L2431: ## **Occurrences of Major Adverse Vascular Events**
L2432: In the core treatment period and extension treatment period until the cutoff date, none of the subjects experienced major adverse vascular events.

L2450: ## 17.3. PNH Pool Safety Results
L2451: The PNH pool included 170 subjects with PNH treated with iptacopan (154 received iptacopan as monotherapy and 15 received iptacopan as add-on therapy) from studies C12302 (APPLY-PNH), C12301 (APPOINT-PNH), C12001B (PNH REP), X2201, X2204, and treatment period 4 of LFG316X2201 study. Note, regarding the PNH pool, the add-on study group only contained events from while the patients were on add-on therapy. These subjects could have also received monotherapy at some point which would be represented in the pooled study arm, because that included both add-on and monotherapy. Therefore, the pooled study group can have more events than the monotherapy and add-on therapy study arms.
L2452: ## Overview of Adverse Events
L2453: The majority of subjects had mild or moderate treatment-emergent adverse events.

L2502: ## **Drug Induced Liver Injury Assessment**
L2503: The majority of liver laboratory abnormalities were related to hemolysis. Both cases of potential Hy's law were related to events of breakthrough hemolysis and not related to iptacopan. Two subjects (b) (6) and (b) (6) had concurrent adverse events of breakthrough hemolysis and LDH levels &gt;1.5 times the ULN. For both subjects, aspartate transferase met the criteria of ≥3 times the ULN, but alanine transferase did not.

L2511: ## 17.4. Deaths
L2512: In the entire development program, there were four deaths in the clinical trials. One subject (Subject [REDACTED](b) (6)) who died was diagnosed with lymphoproliferative disorder, which was related to iptacopan according to the Applicant. When queried regarding the relatedness of iptacopan to lymphoproliferative disorder, the Applicant replied that the relationship could not be excluded because iptacopan is a complement inhibitor and may have had a role due to its ability to inhibit the immune system. Therefore, a conservative stance was taken. Lymphoproliferative disorder can occur in the setting of a dysregulated immune system. The subject died 11 months after discontinuing iptacopan due to sepsis. The exact cause of the lymphoproliferative disorder is difficult to determine.

L2515: Patient details

Contributing event(s)	Comments
PNH patient
(b) (6)
45 years old, female
Day 699	Septic shock,
Lymphoproliferative
disorder	The patient had received eculizumab, prior to and during the study as standard of care at a dose of 1500 mg from Day -227 to Day 168 and 1200 mg from Day 196 to the last dose on Day 696. Lymphocytes at baseline were 0.22 \times 10^9/L (lymphopenia). The patient developed widespread lymphadenopathies and multiple extra-lymphatic nodules approximately 11 months after starting iptacopan 200 mg b.i.d. Subsequently, the patient was diagnosed with B-cell lymphoproliferative disorder, and iptacopan was discontinued (Day 356). The lymphoproliferative disorder was suspected to be related to iptacopan. Eleven months after discontinuation of iptacopan (Day 698), following a cycle of salvage chemotherapy, the patient developed febrile neutropenia, cholangitis and septic shock with fatal outcome.
PNH patient
(b) (6)
78 years old, male
Day 867	General physical health
deterioration,
Penetrating aortic ulcer,
Escherichia bacteremia	The patient was diagnosed with basal cell carcinoma on Day 628 and recovered on Day 637. The patient had penetrating aortic ulcer on Day 765 and received interventions (surgery) – this event was reported as resolved with sequelae on Day 771. The patient received the last dose of iptacopan on Day 779. The patient had Escherichia coli bacteremia on Day 791 and recovered on Day 803 but had general physical health deterioration starting on Day 812 and recurrent E. coli bacteremia on Day 826. The patient was referred to palliative care on Day 841 and died on Day 867. The event was not suspected to be related to iptacopan.
PNH patient
(b) (6)
52 years old, male
Day 504	Squamous cell
carcinoma of the oral
cavity,
Squamous cell
carcinoma of the tongue	The patient had received eculizumab, prior to and during the study as standard of care at a dose of 900 mg until Day 325. The patient developed ulceration of a pre-existing tongue lesion on Day 44 which was histologically confirmed as squamous cell carcinoma (severe, grade 3) of the tongue on Day 100. The patient underwent surgeries on Day 112 and Day 353. The patient went into remission following hemiglossectomy. On Day 402, the patient was readmitted to the hospital due to severe odynophagia. A CT scan on Day 427 showed a new oral/neck lesion of 47x40 mm. Histology assessment on Day 441 confirmed the relapse of the known squamous cell carcinoma (described as human papillomavirus positive). The patient stopped iptacopan treatment on Day 452 and died on Day 504. The event was not suspected to be related to iptacopan.
Renal (C3G) patient
(b) (6)
24 years old, female
Day 501	Cardiac arrhythmia	The C3G patient had pre-existing conditions of anxiety, palpitations, hyperparathyroidism; concomitant medications of methylphenidate (ADRs of tachycardia and arrhythmia), venlafaxine (ADRs of tachycardia and arrhythmia), trazodone (warning for QT prolongation) and medical history of tobacco that are possible confounders for the event. The patient was found dead in her bathroom. The event was not suspected to be related to iptacopan.
Autopsy results concluded cause of death was cardiac arrhythmia based on arterio-nephrosclerosis, clinical history of C3G and hypertension, focal myocardial scar, clinical history of hypocalcemia and history of having been found unresponsive in bathroom.

L2526: ## 22. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L2527: A clinical inspection summary was completed by the Division of Clinical Compliance Evaluation in the Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI) with a final signature date of October 27, 2023. The Applicant and two foreign clinical investigators, Alexander Roeth, M.D., and Regis P. de Latour, M.D., were inspected for study CLNP023C12302.
L2528: OSI concluded that based on the inspection results, the study appears to have been conducted adequately and the study data derived from the above clinical investigator sites and Applicant are considered reliable. The data from study CLNP023C12302, submitted by the Applicant to the Agency for assessment, appear acceptable in support of the proposed indication.

L2534: ## 24. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L2535: ## Postmarketing Requirements
L2536: - Participate in a registry to characterize the long-term safety of iptacopan in adults with paroxysmal nocturnal hemoglobinuria, with up to 5 years of follow-up. Submit yearly safety follow-up data and a summary of the major safety findings for all patients and all serious infections with encapsulated bacteria. The final study report should include an integrated safety dataset and patient-level data, including data on iptacopan dosing, meningococcal, pneumococcal, and *H. influenza* vaccination status, and concomitant medications.

L2654: Diagram illustrating the mechanism of action of Iptacopan targeting the Alternative Complement Pathway.
The diagram shows the Classical pathway and Lectin pathway converging to form C3 convertase (C3(H_2O)Bb), which cleaves C3 into C3a and C3b. C3b is subject to an Amplification loop, where Factor B binds to C3b, forming C3bB. Factor D cleaves C3bB into C3b and Factor B (Bb), which then binds to another C3b molecule, forming C3bBb (C3 convertase). This process is inhibited by Iptacopan, which binds to the Bb domain of Factor B, preventing the formation of C3 convertase.
C3b also leads to C5 convertase (C3bBb), which cleaves C5 into C5a and C5b. C5a binds to C5aR, and C5b initiates the formation of the Membrane Attack Complex (MAC, C6 C7 C8 C9).
Legend: Proteolytic cleavage (indicated by a lightning bolt symbol).
L2655: Diagram illustrating the Classical and Alternative Pathways of complement activation, showing the C3 Convertase Amplification Loop in the Alternative Pathway (AP).
Classical Pathway (Immune complexes: IgG + IgM) or Lectin (Microbial surfaces):
C3 is cleaved by C3 Convertase (C4b C2a).
C3b is generated.
C3b is cleaved by C5 Convertase (C4b C2a C3b).
C5 is cleaved into C5a and C5b.
C5b initiates the formation of the Membrane Attack Complex (MAC), leading to Cell Lysis.
Alternative Pathway (AP):
C3 is cleaved by Factor D (Tick over) into C3a and C3b.
C3b is cleaved by C3 Convertase (C3b Bb).
C3b is cleaved by C5 Convertase (C3b Bb C3b).
C5 is cleaved into C5a and C5b.
C5b initiates the formation of the Membrane Attack Complex (MAC), leading to Cell Lysis.
The diagram highlights the Amplification loop in the AP, where C3b generated by Factor D cleavage feeds back into the C3 Convertase (C3b Bb) step. The drug NVP-LNP023 is shown inhibiting the C3 Convertase step in the AP.
L2656: Figure 3: Study Design – APPLY-PNH. The diagram illustrates the timeline of the study. The Study Period is divided into a Screening Period (D-56 to D1) and a Randomized Treatment Period (D1 to D168). The Treatment Extension Period follows (D168 to D336 EOS). During the Randomized Treatment Period, subjects are randomized into two arms. Arm 1 (Anti-C5 antibody) receives Anti-C5 antibody treatment for Up to 8 weeks, followed by LNP023 200mg b.i.d. for 24 weeks. Arm 2 (Anti-C5 antibody) receives Anti-C5 antibody treatment for 24 weeks, followed by Switch to LNP023 200mg b.i.d. for 24 weeks. The duration of the Randomized Treatment Period is 24 weeks for both arms.
L2657: Diagram illustrating the graphical display of the Multiple Testing Procedure (MTP) for Study CLNP023C12302. The diagram shows a hierarchical structure of hypotheses (H1, H2, H3, H41, H42, H43, H51, H52, H53) and their associated weights and rejection paths.
Legend:
H1: Increase \ge 2 g/dL
H2: Threshold \ge 12 g/dL
H3: Transfusion Avoidance
H41: Change in Hb
H42: FACIT-Fatigue
H43: Reticulocyte counts
H51: LDH
H52: Breakthrough hemolysis
H53: MAVE
The diagram shows the following weights and connections:
H1 and H2 (Primary endpoints) have a weight of 0.15.
H3 (Secondary endpoint) has a weight of 0.45.
H41, H42, and H43 (Secondary endpoints) have a weight of 0.15.
H51, H52, and H53 (Tertiary endpoints) have a weight of 0.05.
Connections between H1 and H2 are weighted 0.10.
Connections between H1 and H3, H2 and H3, H1 and H41, H2 and H41, H1 and H42, H2 and H42, H1 and H43, H2 and H43 are weighted 0.15.
Connections between H3 and H41, H3 and H42, H3 and H43 are weighted 0.15.
Connections between H41 and H42, H41 and H43, H42 and H43 are weighted 0.15.
Connections between H51 and H52, H51 and H53, H52 and H53 are weighted 0.05.
A dashed line indicates a path from H1 to H51, H52, and H53, labeled 0.15.
A path from H3 to H51, H52, and H53 is labeled 1/2 - 2\alpha.

L2668: Figure 6 displays two graphs illustrating the inhibition of C3 cleavage by iptacopan (LNP023).
Graph A: Relative C3d+C3b level
This bar chart compares the relative C3d+C3b level in rats treated with LPS (lipopolysaccharide) and LNP023 (iptacopan) at various doses (30, 20, 10, and 3 mg/kg) or vehicle (veh) compared to PBS (phosphate-buffered saline) control. The PBS control shows a relative level of 1.0. The LPS control shows a relative level of approximately 3.0. LNP023 treatment significantly reduces the relative C3d+C3b level compared to the LPS control. The relative levels for LNP023 doses are: 30 mg/kg (~0.72, p<0.05), 20 mg/kg (~1.0, p<0.01), 10 mg/kg (~1.5, p<0.01), and 3 mg/kg (~2.5, p<0.01). The vehicle (veh) group shows a relative level of 1.0.
Graph B: Relative C3d+C3b level over time
This line graph shows the relative C3d+C3b level over time (0 to 24 hours) post LNP023 p.o. (by mouth). The LPS group shows a peak level of approximately 2.5 at 4 hours, followed by a decrease to a trough of approximately 1.0 at 8 hours, and then a gradual increase back towards 2.5 by 24 hours. The PBS group maintains a stable relative level of approximately 1.0 throughout the 24-hour period.

The X-axis shows three groups: Sheep Control serum + vehicle (low deposition), anti-Fx\alpha1 + vehicle (high deposition), and anti-Fx\alpha1 + LNP023 (60mg) (low deposition). L2670: Figure 11 illustrates the possible metabolic pathway of Iptacopan, showing the conversion of the parent drug (Iptacopan) into various metabolites (M1 through M13, LNP023, M2, M3, M6, M8, M10, M11, M12, M13, M5, M7) across different species (rat, human, dog).
The pathway shows several routes of metabolism, including oxidation, reduction, and conjugation. A major pathway is indicated, leading to metabolites M12 and M13.
Metabolites are labeled with abbreviations indicating the species and matrix of detection: (rh, dH, hH) (rat hepatocytes, dog hepatocytes, human hepatocytes); (rp, nu, rf, rb) (rat plasma, rat urine, rat feces, rat bile); (dp) (dog plasma); (hf) (human feces); (hu) (human urine); (tp) (rat plasma); (hp) (human plasma).
L2671: Flowchart illustrating the elimination pathways of LNP023.
LNP023 is shown at the top, branching into two main pathways:
HEPATIC (Liver): Active uptake (OATP1B1, OATP1B3) leads to:Biliary secretion (P-gp): ~12%
Oxidative metabolism (CYP2C8 and others): ~50%
Non-oxidative metabolism (UGT): ~21%

EXTRAHEPATIC: Leads to:Renal excretion: ~3-5%
Intestinal secretion and/or lung metabolism: ~4%

The overall distribution is approximately 83% HEPATIC and 7-9% EXTRAHEPATIC.

L2675: Figure 16 is a scatter plot showing Dose Normalized AUC_{0-\tau} (in hr \cdot ng/mL/Dose) on the Y-axis (0 to 400) versus Dose (mg) on the X-axis (0 to 250).
The plot displays data for two time points: Day 1 (blue line with triangles) and Day 14 (red line with squares).
On Day 1, AUC_{0-\tau} decreases sharply as the dose increases, starting around 250 and dropping to approximately 100 at 100 mg. On Day 14, AUC_{0-\tau} is consistently lower, starting around 350 and dropping to approximately 130 at 100 mg.

L2677: Figure 18 displays two graphs showing Mean Iptacopan Plasma Concentration-Time Profiles in Japanese Male Subjects for doses of 25 mg, 100 mg, and 400 mg. The left graph is a Linear view, and the right graph is a Semilogarithmic view. Both graphs show concentration (ng/mL) on the y-axis versus time (hr) on the x-axis. The 400 mg dose consistently shows the highest concentration, followed by 100 mg, and then 25 mg. The 400 mg dose also exhibits the longest half-life (T1/2) in the linear view, reaching approximately 1000 ng/mL at 24 hours.

The Y-axis for the SemiLog Scale (right) is Mean LNP023 Concentration (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale from 0.1 to 10000. The top left plot shows IWRES versus TIME (log scale, from 10^{-1} to 10^3). The IWRES values are centered around zero, with a red loess smooth line indicating no systematic trend over time.
The top right plot shows IWRES versus IPRED (from 100 to 3000). The IWRES values are centered around zero, with a red loess smooth line indicating no systematic trend across individual predictions.
The bottom left plot shows the distribution of IWRES. The solid black line represents the density function, and the dashed black line represents the normal distribution. A vertical dashed line is drawn at IWRES = 0.
The bottom right plot shows the IWRES Quantiles versus Standard Normal Quantiles, used for a normality test. The data points generally follow the solid black line (y=x), suggesting approximate normality of the residuals.
L2714: Figure 52 displays two scatter plots comparing observed data (Y-axis: Observations) against model predictions (X-axis) for LDH (lactate dehydrogenase). The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 100 to 3000. The X-axis is also logarithmic, ranging from 100 to 3000.
The left plot shows Population predictions (X-axis) versus Observations (Y-axis). The right plot shows Individual predictions (X-axis) versus Observations (Y-axis).
In both plots, black points represent non-censored data, and a red line represents a loess smooth. A solid black line represents the identity line (y=x).
The plots illustrate the performance of the exposure-response model for LDH, showing that the predictions generally follow the observed data trend, especially at higher values.
L2715: Figure 53 displays six Visual Predictive Checks (VPCs) for Lactate Dehydrogenase (LDH) concentration (U/L) versus Time (days) for the Exposure-Response Model for Hemoglobin (Hb). The plots are stratified by Study and Treatment Arm.
The Y-axis represents LDH concentration (U/L) on a logarithmic scale (100 to 3000). The X-axis represents Time (days) from -200 to 800.
The six panels show:
CLNP023C12301: LNP023 200 mg
CLNP023C12302: Anti-C5 - LNP023 200 mg
CLNP023C12302: LNP023 200 mg
CLNP023X2201: SOC + LNP023 200 mg
CLNP023X2201: SOC + LNP023 50 mg
CLNP023X2204: LNP023 25-100 mg
CLNP023X2204: LNP023 50-200 mg
The legend indicates:
Solid and dashed lines: quantiles (solid black line: 50%, dashed black lines: 5% and 95%) of the observed data within bin.
Red solid line: median model prediction.
Dashed blue lines: 5th and 95th quantiles of model prediction.
Shaded areas: 95% confidence intervals for the percentiles.
Dots: observations.
Percentiles are plotted at the median time point in the bins.

L2716: Figure 54 displays two panels showing the exposure-response relationship of LDH (Lactate Dehydrogenase) at steady state for PNH subjects based on prior treatment status. The top panel shows LDH (U/L) versus Steady state Caverage (ng/mL) on a log scale. The left panel is for PNH experienced subjects, and the right panel is for PNH naive subjects. The LDH level decreases as Caverage increases. The bottom panel shows Dose (mg BID) versus Steady state Caverage (ng/mL) on a log scale, showing box plots for different doses (25, 50, 100, 200 mg BID). Vertical dashed lines indicate the EC90 of LDH, and vertical dotted lines indicate median steady state Caverage per dose level.
L2717: Figure 55 displays the NPDE Panel for the Exposure-Response Model for Hb, consisting of three plots:
Top Plot (NPDE Versus Time): Shows NPDE (Y-axis, ranging from -4 to 4) versus TIME (X-axis, logarithmic scale from 10^{-1} to 10^{3}). A red loess smooth line is centered around NPDE=0. Horizontal dashed lines indicate \pm 2 Standardized Residuals.
Middle Plot (NPDE Versus PRED): Shows NPDE (Y-axis, ranging from -4 to 4) versus PRED (X-axis, ranging from 60 to 100). A red loess smooth line is centered around NPDE=0. Horizontal dashed lines indicate \pm 2 Standardized Residuals.
Bottom Plot (NPDE Quantiles Versus Standard Normal Quantile): Shows NPDE Quantiles (Y-axis, ranging from -4 to 4) versus Standard Normal Quantile (X-axis, ranging from -4 to 2). The data points closely follow the solid black line (y=x), indicating a good fit to the standard normal distribution.

L2719: Figure 57 displays two scatter plots comparing Hb (hemoglobin) observations against predictions from an Exposure-Response Model. L2721: Figure 59 displays the Exposure-Response (ER) relationship for Hemoglobin (Hb) at steady state, stratified by PNH Prior Treatment Status (PNH experienced and PNH naive). The figure consists of two panels, each showing two plots: Hb (g/L) versus Steady state Coverage (ng/mL) (top row) and Dose (mg BID) versus Steady state Coverage (ng/mL) (bottom row).
The top row plots show Hb concentration (Y-axis, 90 to 150 g/L) versus Steady state Coverage (X-axis, logarithmic scale, 1 to 10000 ng/mL). A solid line represents the median ER prediction, and a shaded grey area represents the 90% of the population. Vertical dashed lines indicate the EC90 of LDH, and vertical dotted lines indicate the median steady state Coverage per dose level.
The bottom row plots show Dose (mg BID) (Y-axis, 25 to 200 mg BID) versus Steady state Coverage (X-axis, logarithmic scale, 1 to 10000 ng/mL). Box plots illustrate the distribution of steady state Coverage for different doses.
